- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Self-Representation Alignment (SRA), a method to improve the training of diffusion transformers (DiTs and SiTs) by enabling them to provide representation guidance to themselves during generative training. Unlike existing methods that rely on additional representation training frameworks or pre-trained models, SRA aligns the output latent representations of the diffusion transformer at different noise levels. Specifically, it aligns earlier layers with higher noise to later layers with lower noise through a self-distillation process, enhancing overall representation learning without introducing external components. The paper shows that applying SRA to DiTs and SiTs yields consistent performance improvements and achieves comparable results to methods that heavily rely on external representation priors.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in the *simplicity* and *self-contained nature* of SRA. The idea of using a diffusion transformer's inherent denoising process for self-guidance is novel. Existing approaches required either pre-trained models or more complex, auxiliary training objectives. SRA offers a more streamlined and efficient approach. However, the use of EMA (Exponential Moving Average) for the teacher network and aligning features across layers does have some conceptual overlap with self-distillation and related techniques used in other areas of machine learning. The core novelty lies in adapting these concepts specifically within the context of diffusion transformers to leverage their unique generative process.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of SRA is significant due to its practical advantages. It simplifies the training pipeline for diffusion transformers by eliminating the need for external representation guidance. This makes it easier to implement and use, particularly for researchers with limited computational resources or access to large pre-trained models. The consistent performance improvements across different architectures and sizes demonstrate the robustness of the method. The fact that it achieves comparable or better performance than methods using powerful external priors is also a strong result. However, the ImageNet results could be improved.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Simplicity:** SRA is conceptually simple and easy to implement.
    *   **Efficiency:** It does not require additional data or pre-training of external models, reducing computational costs.
    *   **Robustness:**  It demonstrates consistent performance gains across different architectures (DiTs, SiTs) and model sizes.
    *   **Strong results:** It achieves results comparable to methods using complex representation learning frameworks or strong external priors, but with fewer components.
    *   **Thorough experiments:** The paper provides detailed ablation studies that systematically evaluate the different components of SRA.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited theoretical grounding:** The paper lacks a strong theoretical analysis of why SRA works. While it offers an intuitive explanation based on the coarse-to-fine denoising process, a more formal justification would strengthen the contribution.
    *   **Dependency on EMA:** SRA relies on an EMA teacher network, a form of self-distillation. While not a "external" component in the same way a pre-trained model is, EMA still adds a layer of complexity and specific parameter choices.
    *   **Incremental improvement:** While the method shows good performance and provides a novel idea, the performance gains with SRA are not revolutionary. SOTA performance on complex and higher resolution data is still a challenge.
    *   **ImageNet-centric evaluation**: The bulk of the experiments are done on ImageNet, which is now almost a "solved" dataset. Stronger evaluations on more complex generation tasks, like those involving text-to-image or high-resolution generation, would strengthen the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:** SRA is likely to influence future research on diffusion transformer training by providing a more efficient and self-contained approach to representation learning. It also encourages further exploration of the intrinsic representation capabilities of generative models and how they can be leveraged for improved performance. It opens the door for further refinement and adaptation of the self-guidance mechanism, leading to new and improved methods for diffusion model training.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and practical approach to improving diffusion transformer training with self-representation alignment. While relying on a well-established EMA technique, SRA adapts that concept in a simple yet effective manner. The code is made available, which promotes widespread adoption. The thorough experimental evaluation demonstrates its robustness and effectiveness. However, the lack of deep theoretical analysis, incremental performance gains, and the ImageNet-centric evaluation prevent it from achieving a higher score. Nonetheless, SRA is a solid contribution that simplifies and improves the training process for diffusion transformers.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### R1-Reward: Training Multimodal Reward Model Through Stable Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02835v1)
- **Authors**: Yi-Fan Zhang, Xingyu Lu, Xiao Hu, Chaoyou Fu, Bin Wen, Tianke Zhang, Changyi Liu, Kaiyu Jiang, Kaibing Chen, Kaiyu Tang, Haojie Ding, Jiankang Chen, Fan Yang, Zhang Zhang, Tingting Gao, Liang Wang
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Reward Models (MRMs) play a crucial role in enhancing the performance of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). While recent advancements have primarily focused on improving the model structure and training data of MRMs, there has been limited exploration into the effectiveness of long-term reasoning capabilities for reward modeling and how to activate these capabilities in MRMs. In this paper, we explore how Reinforcement Learning (RL) can be used to improve reward modeling. Specifically, we reformulate the reward modeling problem as a rule-based RL task. However, we observe that directly applying existing RL algorithms, such as Reinforce++, to reward modeling often leads to training instability or even collapse due to the inherent limitations of these algorithms. To address this issue, we propose the StableReinforce algorithm, which refines the training loss, advantage estimation strategy, and reward design of existing RL methods. These refinements result in more stable training dynamics and superior performance. To facilitate MRM training, we collect 200K preference data from diverse datasets. Our reward model, R1-Reward, trained using the StableReinforce algorithm on this dataset, significantly improves performance on multimodal reward modeling benchmarks. Compared to previous SOTA models, R1-Reward achieves a $8.4\%$ improvement on the VL Reward-Bench and a $14.3\%$ improvement on the Multimodal Reward Bench. Moreover, with more inference compute, R1-Reward's performance is further enhanced, highlighting the potential of RL algorithms in optimizing MRMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces R1-Reward, a multimodal reward model (MRM) trained using a novel reinforcement learning (RL) approach called StableReinforce.  The authors argue that existing MRMs haven't fully exploited the potential of RL to improve long-term reasoning capabilities for reward modeling, a crucial aspect for improving multimodal large language models (MLLMs). They reformulate reward modeling as a rule-based RL task but observe training instability when applying standard RL algorithms. To address this, StableReinforce refines the training loss, advantage estimation, and reward design. They collect a dataset of 200K preference examples and demonstrate that R1-Reward achieves state-of-the-art performance on several MRM benchmarks, showing significant improvements in accuracy and highlighting the potential of RL for MRM optimization. The model exhibits strong inference time scalability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its *application of reinforcement learning to the problem of training multimodal reward models*, coupled with a novel algorithm designed for *Stable Reinforcement Learning*. While the general concept of using RL for language model alignment isn't new, the specific focus on *reward modeling for MLLMs* represents a valuable contribution. The core innovation, StableReinforce, addresses a practical challenge in applying RL directly to this task: training instability. The refinement of existing RL methods (loss clipping, advantage normalization) is incremental, but their combination tailored for MRMs appears effective. The introduction of a consistency reward using an MLLM referee to ensure alignment between reasoning and output is also a positive contribution.

**Significance:**  The paper is significant because it addresses a crucial aspect of improving MLLMs: better reward models.  Improved reward models translate into better alignment with human preferences and can lead to more robust and reliable MLLMs. The empirical results demonstrate a clear improvement over existing methods on standard benchmarks, substantiating the effectiveness of their approach.  The inference-time scaling experiments are also relevant, as they suggest that R1-Reward can leverage increased computational resources to achieve even better performance.  The authors also contribute a new large multimodal preference dataset. However, the significance is somewhat tempered by the fact that the core RL improvements are built upon existing algorithms (Reinforce++) and the impact largely relies on its demonstrated effectiveness on current MRM datasets.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of training stable and effective MRMs using RL.
*   **Novel algorithm:** StableReinforce represents a practical and effective solution for the specific challenges of RL-based MRM training.
*   **Strong empirical results:** The experiments convincingly demonstrate the superiority of R1-Reward over existing methods on multiple benchmarks.
*   **Detailed ablation studies:** The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component of StableReinforce.
*   **Relevant dataset contribution:**  The released dataset is a valuable resource for the community.
*   **Inference-time analysis:** Analysis of model scalability across multiple inference samples is insightful.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental algorithmic improvement:**  The algorithmic improvements in StableReinforce, while effective, are largely refinements of existing techniques.
*   **Limited analysis of MLLM Referee's bias:** Analysis of MLLM's effect of using "Aha moment" is not deeply analysed.
*   **Dependency on GPT-40 for data generation:** The dependence on GPT-40 for generating thinking processes might introduce biases and limit the diversity of the training data.
*   **Generalization:** The model's generalization to out-of-distribution or more complex multimodal tasks might be limited.
*   **Potential for Negative Societal Impacts:** RL-based models can sometimes reinforce problematic biases or encourage harmful behavior if not carefully controlled.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7**.  The paper makes a valuable contribution by demonstrating a practically effective approach to training multimodal reward models using RL. The StableReinforce algorithm addresses a real challenge in the field, and the empirical results are compelling. However, the core algorithmic novelty is somewhat limited, and the reliance on external MLLM's biases for thinking processes for data generation creates some constraints in practical implementation. While a solid and useful piece of work, a perfect score is not achieved due to the incremental nature of some aspects of novelty. Despite the incremental nature of the algorithmic contributions, the comprehensive study, strong empirical results, and practical insights provided make it a solid step forward for MLLM reward modeling with good impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 0/10

### Scenethesis: A Language and Vision Agentic Framework for 3D Scene Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02836v1)
- **Authors**: Lu Ling, Chen-Hsuan Lin, Tsung-Yi Lin, Yifan Ding, Yu Zeng, Yichen Sheng, Yunhao Ge, Ming-Yu Liu, Aniket Bera, Zhaoshuo Li
- **Abstract**: Synthesizing interactive 3D scenes from text is essential for gaming, virtual reality, and embodied AI. However, existing methods face several challenges. Learning-based approaches depend on small-scale indoor datasets, limiting the scene diversity and layout complexity. While large language models (LLMs) can leverage diverse text-domain knowledge, they struggle with spatial realism, often producing unnatural object placements that fail to respect common sense. Our key insight is that vision perception can bridge this gap by providing realistic spatial guidance that LLMs lack. To this end, we introduce Scenethesis, a training-free agentic framework that integrates LLM-based scene planning with vision-guided layout refinement. Given a text prompt, Scenethesis first employs an LLM to draft a coarse layout. A vision module then refines it by generating an image guidance and extracting scene structure to capture inter-object relations. Next, an optimization module iteratively enforces accurate pose alignment and physical plausibility, preventing artifacts like object penetration and instability. Finally, a judge module verifies spatial coherence. Comprehensive experiments show that Scenethesis generates diverse, realistic, and physically plausible 3D interactive scenes, making it valuable for virtual content creation, simulation environments, and embodied AI research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Scenethesis: A Language and Vision Agentic Framework for 3D Scene Generation" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Scenethesis, a novel, training-free agentic framework for generating interactive 3D scenes from text prompts. It addresses the limitations of existing methods, which either rely on small-scale, limited datasets or struggle with spatial realism when leveraging large language models (LLMs).  Scenethesis integrates LLM-based scene planning with vision-guided layout refinement. The framework involves:

1.  **LLM-based Scene Planning:** An LLM generates a coarse scene layout based on the text prompt.
2.  **Vision-Guided Refinement:** A vision module refines the layout, using image generation, depth estimation, and structural extraction to capture inter-object relationships and visual realism.
3.  **Physics-Aware Optimization:** This module enforces accurate pose alignment and physical plausibility through semantic correspondence matching and SDF-based physical constraints, preventing object penetration and instability.
4.  **Scene Judge:** A module validates the generated scene for spatial coherence.

The authors demonstrate that Scenethesis generates more diverse, realistic, and physically plausible 3D interactive scenes compared to state-of-the-art methods, making it valuable for virtual content creation, simulation environments, and embodied AI research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies in its *training-free* agentic approach that effectively integrates LLMs with vision foundation models and physical constraints. Combining LLM-based planning with vision-guided refinement is a significant step forward.  The iterative optimization using semantic correspondence and SDF-based collision avoidance is also a novel contribution. However, similar works exists exploring this topic.
*   **Significance:** Synthesizing interactive 3D scenes from text is crucial for various applications, including gaming, VR/AR, and robotics. The paper tackles a key challenge in the field – generating realistic and physically plausible scenes without relying on extensive training data.  The ability to generate diverse indoor and outdoor environments is also a valuable contribution. However, the framework seems more suitable for smaller scales. It may encounter challenges in larger scene settings.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The architecture is well-designed and clearly explained, with a logical flow from text prompt to 3D scene.
    *   The integration of multiple modalities (language, vision, physics) is a strong point.
    *   The training-free approach makes it more adaptable and scalable than methods relying on labeled 3D datasets.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of Scenethesis in terms of diversity, realism, physical plausibility, and interactivity.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on a retrieval-based approach for 3D asset selection might limit the diversity and creativity of the generated scenes. Generative 3D models are not used.
    *   The vision module's performance depends on the quality of the image generation model.
    *   The physical plausibility optimization, while effective, might introduce artifacts or unnatural object arrangements in some cases.
    *   Limited experiments are conducted. Results should be reinforced.
    *   The framework seems more suitable for smaller scales. It may encounter challenges in larger scene settings.

*   **Potential Impact:** Scenethesis has the potential to significantly impact the field of 3D scene generation by providing a more flexible, realistic, and physically plausible approach. It could be used to create virtual environments for gaming, training, and simulation, as well as to improve the performance of embodied AI agents.
*   **Rigorousity of Justification:** The work has been rigorously justified in both qualitative and quantitative results, making it a reliable result.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

While the paper presents a novel and significant contribution to 3D scene generation, it's not groundbreaking enough to warrant a score of 9 or 10. The novelty of the approach is notable, but the reliance on retrieval-based asset selection and image generation models imposes some limitations. Further improvements in these areas, as well as addressing the potential drawbacks of the physical plausibility optimization, could elevate the paper's impact.
